It's just a temporary thing, really. I hope that answers your question okay? I'm trying really hard to give you the right details, I just don't have a better way to describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have gaps in memory for personal events or conversations, beyond briefly forgetting what to do at work?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I remember conversations with my family and friends just fine. I might forget where I put my keys every now and then, like anyone else, but nothing big like missing whole events or anything. \n\nI promise my memory for personal things is still really good. It's really just... just those little work hiccups I mentioned. I hope that makes sense? I just really don't want you to think my whole mind is going bad or that I'm completely falling apart, because outside of those blank moments, I'm usually very reliable. I don't feel detached or like things are fake or anything scary like that. It's really just a normal blank moment, like... like when you walk into a room and forget why you went in there? It's just the specific steps that slip my mind for a second. I hope that's the right kind of answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, because I'm really not having any sort of out-of-body experiences or anything weird. I'm just a little forgetful sometimes. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Despite these lapses, are you still able to perform your job and manage your usual daily activities independently?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I try really, really hard to keep everything going, I promise I do. But, if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest so you can help me—I guess... I haven't been as reliable at work as I usually am. \n\nIt just got to the point where I couldn't finish the road-closure paperwork, and... well, I just couldn't trust myself with the traffic-safety steps. I would feel just terrible if anyone got hurt because of my silly mistakes, you know? So work has been hard. \n\nAnd then, outside of work... I guess I sort of pulled away from my choir, and my volunteering. Just to rest, really! But I ended up pulling away from my family, too. I didn't mean to worry them, but they noticed I wasn't doing my usual things, and... well, they actually felt like they had to bring me in here for help. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to admit all that. I'm usually a very independent, capable person, I swear. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up on everything or being lazy. Does that answer your question okay? I'll just be standing there, and the thought just slips away. I try really hard to stay calm and just wait for it to come back. I hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of this than it is. I'm trying to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank spells happen only at work, or do they also happen in other places?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's mostly at work, which is why my supervisor noticed. But, um... now that you mention it, it does happen at home sometimes, too. Just little things, you know? Like I'll be in the kitchen and completely forget what I was doing. But the main reason I actually came in today is because of how it's affecting my job. Like I said before, I'll just be looking at the road-closure forms, or holding my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just cannot remember the steps. Steps I've done for years. It's so silly, really. I'm sorry to keep repeating myself about the forms and the paddle, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is it okay that I brought that up again?\n\nDoctor: Have these memory lapses been gradually getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if I'd say they're getting *worse*, exactly? I really don't want it to sound like I'm completely losing my mind or anything. It's just... well, it's been happening enough lately that it scared me into making an appointment. Like I mentioned, I just came in because my mind keeps going blank at the worst possible times. I'll be looking right at those road-closure forms, or holding my stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly cannot remember the steps I've done for years. It's just a little hiccup, really, but it's embarrassing when it happens on the job. I hope I'm explaining it okay? I'm trying not to make it sound like a bigger deal than it is, but I just want to do my job right.\n\nDoctor: When the blank passes, do you remember what you were supposed to do, or is that part of the event still missing from your memory?\n\nPatient: Um... usually it does come back after a little bit. I know I sound like a broken record, I'm so sorry for bringing it up again. I just... I really want to make sure I'm explaining the main reason I came in properly. It's just that my mind keeps going blank at the absolute worst times, and for that moment, when I'm looking at the road-closure forms or holding the stop-slow paddle, I just suddenly cannot remember those steps I've done for years. \n\nBut then, yeah, after a minute or two it usually kind of clicks back into place and I remember what I'm supposed to be doing. It's just a temporary thing, really. I hope that answers your question okay? I'm trying really hard to give you the right details, I just don't have a better way to describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have gaps in memory for personal events or conversations, beyond briefly forgetting what to do at work?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I remember conversations with my family and friends just fine. I might forget where I put my keys every now and then, like anyone else, but nothing big like missing whole events or anything. \n\nI promise my memory for personal things is still really good. It's really just... just those little work hiccups I mentioned. I hope that makes sense? I just really don't want you to think my whole mind is going bad or that I'm completely falling apart, because outside of those blank moments, I'm usually very reliable. Am I answering your questions okay?\n\nDoctor: During these blank spells, do you feel detached from yourself or from your surroundings, as if things are unreal?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't feel detached or like things are fake or anything scary like that. It's really just a normal blank moment, like... like when you walk into a room and forget why you went in there? Just a little more inconvenient because of where I'm standing. \n\nI'm definitely still right there, in the moment, looking at the cars and the road. It's just the specific steps that slip my mind for a second. I hope that's the right kind of answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, because I'm really not having any sort of out-of-body experiences or anything weird. I'm just a little forgetful sometimes. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Despite these lapses, are you still able to perform your job and manage your usual daily activities independently?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I try really, really hard to keep everything going, I promise I do. But, if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest so you can help me—I guess... I haven't been as reliable at work as I usually am. \n\nIt just got to the point where I couldn't finish the road-closure paperwork, and... well, I just couldn't trust myself with the traffic-safety steps. I would feel just terrible if anyone got hurt because of my silly mistakes, you know? So work has been hard. \n\nAnd then, outside of work... I guess I sort of pulled away from my choir, and my volunteering. Just to rest, really! But I ended up pulling away from my family, too. I didn't mean to worry them, but they noticed I wasn't doing my usual things, and... well, they actually felt like they had to bring me in here for help. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to admit all that. I'm usually a very independent, capable person, I swear. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up on everything or being lazy. Does that answer your question okay? I really want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you need another person to help you manage your usual daily tasks, such as paying bills, taking medications, or preparing meals?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, um... definitely not. I can still handle all of that on my own, I promise. I make sure my bills are paid on time, and... and I can still put together my meals just fine. I really don't want to be a burden on my family for the basic everyday things, you know? \n\nSo I make sure I stay on top of all that. I don't want you to think I'm completely helpless or anything, because I'm really not. I'm still trying my very best to take care of myself at home, even if work has been a little much lately. I hope that shows I'm still capable? Did I answer that right for you?\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor ever identified a medical or neurological condition that might be causing these memory problems?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. No doctor has ever said anything like that. I've always been very healthy, really. I mean, I go to my checkups and everything is always perfectly fine. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think there's something seriously wrong with my brain or anything scary like that. It's just... well, just these little slips lately. But no medical conditions or neurological things, I promise. I try to take very good care of my health. Is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope that helps rule out anything too terrible.\n\nDoctor: Do you still independently manage complex everyday responsibilities, such as finances, medications, appointments, and transportation?\n\nPatient: Um, yes, I think we just talked about that a little bit, didn't we? I... I definitely still manage all those things on my own. I pay my bills, I get myself to my appointments, and I figure out how to get where I need to go. \n\nI'm very careful about my responsibilities, I promise. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm falling apart completely. I know I mentioned work has been a struggle, but my home life and my finances... I still have a good handle on all of that. I really try to be responsible and not rely on my family for everything. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you needed someone else to take over any of your usual responsibilities, such as managing finances, medications, appointments, or transportation?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, no one has had to take over anything for me. I think... well, I know we've touched on this a few times now, and I'm so sorry if I'm not being clear enough. I really am doing okay with all of those things. \n\nI make sure I handle my own money, and I get myself to the doctor, and take my medicine exactly like I'm supposed to. I don't want to be a burden on anyone, so I just... I push through and make sure it all gets done. I promise I'm still very capable of managing my own life. I really hope I'm answering this the way you need me to? I just want to reassure you that I'm not completely helpless.\n\nDoctor: Are you currently able to perform your work duties independently, or does someone need to supervise you or take over parts of the job?\n\nPatient: Well, um...
